j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{+}
, and
i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{+}
and
j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}
since two original training data will give two mixed data.

In particular, note that
\ell_{i,j}^{(t)}=\Theta(1)
for these two types of data, we can immediately get that there is a constant number of patches that satisfy
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}\geq 1-\lambda
, while the remaining patches
p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})
satisfy
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}\leq\alpha
. Therefore, we can follow the same proof technique as that for the data
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,+}
and get that for all
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}
,

\displaystyle\ell_{k,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle=\ell_{k,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v})}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}+\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})\backslash\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v})}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}=\Theta(1).

(B.6)

Analysis on the data
i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}
and
j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{-}
.

In this part, we will handle data
\mathbf{x}_{i,j}
and
\mathbf{x}_{j,i}
together. Different from the previous cases where the loss derivatives
\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}
are positive, here the loss derivative
\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}
will become negative for
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{-,+}
. Particularly, for any
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}
, we have
(j,i)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}
, then

\displaystyle\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{j,i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})}\big[[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}-[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}\big]+\big[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\big]\cdot\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})}\big[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\big]^{2},

(B.7)

where we use the fact that
\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})=\mathcal{P}_{j,i}(\mathbf{v})
and
\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle=\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})
.
Recall that the neural network output is upper bounded by
\zeta
, then it is easy to see

\displaystyle|\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}|=|\lambda-0.5\pm O(\zeta)+0.5-\lambda\pm O(\zeta)|=O(\zeta).

Besides, note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)}=\lambda\mathbf{x}_{i}^{(p)}+(1-\lambda)\mathbf{x}_{j}^{(p)},\quad\mathbf{x}_{j,i}^{(p)}+(1-\lambda)\mathbf{x}_{i}^{(p)}+\lambda\mathbf{x}_{j}^{(p)}.

Then we will also define
\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v})
as the set of patches with common feature. Note that
\mathbf{x}_{j}^{(p)}
does not have the common feature patch since
j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{-}
, we can immediately get that
\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v})=\mathcal{P}_{i}^{*}(\mathbf{v})
, where
\mathcal{P}_{i}^{*}(\mathbf{v})
denotes the set of common feature patches of
\mathbf{x}_{i}
. Besides, it is also clear that all data patches in
\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})
only contain the feature noise
\alpha\mathbf{v}
. Then it follows that

\displaystyle\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})}\big[[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}-[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\Theta(1)\cdot\bigg[\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v})}[\lambda^{2}-(1-\lambda)^{2}]+\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})\backslash\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v})}\big[[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}-[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}\big]\bigg]

\displaystyle=\Theta(1)\pm O(b\alpha^{2})

\displaystyle=\Theta(1).

Similarly, we can also get
\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})}[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}]^{2}=\Theta(1)
. Therefore, putting everything to (
B.3.1
), we can finally obtain the following

\displaystyle\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{j,i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle=\Theta(1)\pm\Theta(1)\cdot O(\zeta)=\Theta(1).

Analysis on the data
i,j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{-}
In this case, we can observe that there is no common feature patches in
\mathbf{x}_{i}
and
\mathbf{x}_{j}
, while the vector
\mathbf{v}
will only appear in at most
2b
patches of
\mathbf{x}_{i,j}
in the form of feature noise. Therefore, we have
|\Theta_{i,j}^{(p)}|\in[(1-\lambda)\alpha,\alpha]
for at most
2b
patches and the remaining patches will give
|\Theta_{i,j}^{(p)}|=0
. Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\ell_{k,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\bigg|=\bigg|\ell_{k,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}\bigg|=O(b\alpha^{2})=o\bigg(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\bigg).

Completing the analysis for
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})
.
Now we are able to complete the analysis on
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})
based on (
B.4
):

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\bigg[\sum_{(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,+}}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{-,+}}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i,j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{-}}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\bigg[\Theta(1)\cdot|{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,+}|+\Theta(1)\cdot|{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}|+\Theta(1)\cdot|{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{-,+}|

\displaystyle\qquad\pm o\bigg(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\bigg)\cdot|{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{-,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{-,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{-,-}|\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\bigg[\Theta(n^{2})\pm o\bigg(\frac{n^{2}}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\Theta(1).

Proof for
\gamma_{k}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})
.

The next step is to characterize
\gamma_{k}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})
. We will split the entire mixed training dataset into the following classes:

•

i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}
and
j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-}
, and
i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-}
and
j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}
, i.e.,
{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}
.

•

all
(i,j)\not\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}
.

We first recall the formula of
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})
(see Proposition
B.2
):

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle.

(B.8)

Analysis on the data
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}
.

Since
{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}
are symmetric: i.e., for any
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}
, we have
(j,i)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}
and vise versa. Then we will handle data
\mathbf{x}_{i,j}
and
\mathbf{x}_{j,i}
together by studying the following quantity:

\displaystyle*

\displaystyle:=\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{j,i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}).

Note that we will only consider the patch that contains both
\mathbf{u}
and
\mathbf{v}
. Then consider a data patch
\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)}
satisfy this condition:
\mathbf{x}_{i}^{(p)}=\alpha_{i}\mathbf{v}
and
\mathbf{x}_{j}^{(p)}=\alpha_{j}\mathbf{u}
, where
\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}\in\{\alpha,1\}
, which further leads to
\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)}=\lambda\alpha_{i}\mathbf{v}+(1-\lambda)\alpha_{j}\mathbf{u}
and
\mathbf{x}_{j,i}^{(p)}=\lambda\alpha_{j}\mathbf{u}+(1-\lambda)\alpha_{i}\mathbf{v}
. Accordingly, it further gives

\displaystyle\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})=(1-\lambda)\alpha_{j}\cdot\alpha_{i}\lambda_{i}=\lambda\alpha_{j}\cdot(1-\lambda)\alpha_{i}=\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}).

Additionally, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})
, we have at most
\Theta(1)
among them satisfy
\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})=\Theta(1)
and at most
\Theta(1)
among them satisfy
\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})=\Theta(1)
, while the remaining, with size at most
2b
, can only give
\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}),\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})=\Theta(\alpha)
. This implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})=O(1)+O(\alpha)+O(b\alpha^{2})=O(1).

Therefore, applying the above equations, we can get that

\displaystyle*

\displaystyle=\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})}\big[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})-\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\big]+\big[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\big]\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})

\displaystyle=\big[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\big]\cdot O(1).

Further note that in the initial phase we have
\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}=O(\zeta)
, we consequently get

\displaystyle|*|=\bigg|\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{j,i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\bigg|=O(\zeta).

Analysis on the remaining data
(i,j)\not\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}
.

In this case, we note that there are no data patches that satisfy
\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})=\Theta(1)
and
\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})=\Theta(1)
simultaneously. Therefore, for any data
\mathbf{x}_{i,j}
, there will exist at most
\Theta(1)
patches that satisfy
\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})=\alpha
and at most
2b
patches satisfying
\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})=\alpha^{2}
, while the remaining patches will give
\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})=0
. Therefore, we can get that

\displaystyle\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})=\Theta(1)\cdot\alpha+2b\alpha^{2}=O(\alpha),

where the last equality follows from the setting of the data distribution that
b\alpha<1
.

Completing the analysis for
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})
.
By (
B.8
) and using the fact that
|\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}|\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\bigg|\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\bigg|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\cdot\big[|{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}|\cdot O(\zeta)+\big(n^{2}-|{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0,0}^{-,+}|\big)\cdot O(\alpha)\big]

\displaystyle=O(\zeta+\alpha).

Proof for
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})
.

We then tend to characterize
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})
. We will consider the following two classes of data:

•

(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}

•

all
(i,j)\not\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}
.

Analysis on the data
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}

First, it is easy to see that with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
, we have
|{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}|=O(\rho n^{2})
.
For this class of data, with probability
\Theta(1/P)
we have the data
\mathbf{x}_{i,j}^{(p)}
has a constant number of patches that satisfy
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\Theta(1)
. Besides, by Lemma
B.1
, we have with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
, there are
\Theta(b\rho n^{2}/P)
patches are the mixture of
\alpha\mathbf{v}
and
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
, leading to
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\Theta(\alpha)
. The remaining patches will give
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=0
. Combine the above results, we can get

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\Theta\bigg(\frac{\rho n^{2}}{P}\bigg)+\Theta\bigg(\frac{b\alpha\rho n^{2}}{P}\bigg)=\Theta\bigg(\frac{\rho n^{2}}{P}\bigg)

where we use the fact that
b\alpha=o(1)
.

Analysis on the remaining data
Particular, we will only consider the data
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,-}
since otherwise there is no data containing the rare feature vector
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
. Moreover, note that for this class of data we only have
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=O(\alpha)
since there is no data consisting of common feature patch (but only contain feature noise
\alpha\mathbf{v}
). Therefore, similar to the previous analysis, we can get that, by Lemma
B.1
, with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
, there are
\Theta(b\rho n^{2}/P)
patches that give
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\Theta(\alpha)
, which consequently leads to

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,-}}\bigg|\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\bigg|=O\bigg(\frac{b\alpha\rho n^{2}}{P}\bigg).

Completing the analysis for
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})
.
Completing the previous analysis, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})=\Theta\bigg(\frac{\rho}{P}\bigg)\pm O\bigg(\frac{b\alpha\rho}{P}\bigg)=\Theta\bigg(\frac{\rho}{P}\bigg).

Proof for
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})
.

Regarding the coefficient
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})
, we consider two cases (1) mixup between
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{v}
; (2) mixup between
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
\alpha\mathbf{v}
. Then it can be seen that the first cases cover the data
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{-,+}
and
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{+,-}
, which is equivalent to the dataset
\{(i,j),(j,i):(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,-}\}
. Therefore, we will handle the data
(i,j)
and
(j,i)
together in this case. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})

\displaystyle=\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\big[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})-\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\big]+\big[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\big]\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}).

It is clear that the first term on the R.H.S. of the above equation is zero since in case (1)

\displaystyle\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})=\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})=\lambda(1-\lambda).

Regarding the second term, we can use Lemma
B.1
and get that the number of patches falling in case (1) is
\Theta(\rho n^{2}/P)
. Then using the fact that
|\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}|=O(\zeta)
can lead to the final bound for case (1).

Regarding case (2), we can follow the analysis for
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})
, which relies on the fact that
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\Theta(\alpha)
. Therefore, we can finally get

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0,1}^{-,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,-}}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\bigg|

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\frac{\rho}{P}\bigg)\cdot\Theta(\zeta)+O\bigg(\frac{b\alpha\rho}{P}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\Theta\bigg(\frac{\zeta\rho}{P}\bigg),

where we use the fact that
\zeta=\omega(b\alpha)
.

Proof for
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v})
.

Finally, we will study
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v})
. Recall its formula in (
B.3
) we can get

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v})=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}).

Then it can be seen that the noise vector
\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}
will appear in
2n-1
mixup data patches. By Lemma
B.1
, we have with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
,
\Theta(1/P)
fraction of them are mixed with
\mathbf{v}
and
O(b/P)
fraction of them are mixed with
\alpha\mathbf{v}
. Therefore, we can get that

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v})=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{p=q,i=s||p=q,j=s}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})}_{I_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{p\neq q||i\neq s,j\neq s}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})}_{I_{2}},

where it holds that

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\cdot\big[\Theta(n/P)+\Theta(b\alpha/P)\big]=\Theta\bigg(\frac{1}{Pn}\bigg),

and

\displaystyle|I_{2}|\leq\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{P}{d^{1/2}}\bigg),

where we use the fact that
b\alpha=o(1)
and
\theta_{i,j}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})=\widetilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
for all
i\neq s
and
j\neq s
. This further implies that

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v})|=O\bigg(\frac{1}{Pn}\bigg)

since we have assumed that
d\geq P^{4}n^{2}
.
∎

We can also get a similar result for the learning of common feature
\mathbf{u}
.

Lemma B.4
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta=o\big(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\big)
, then recalling the update form in Proposition
B.2
, we have for any
r\in[m]
,
q\in[P]
, and
s\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u})=\Theta(1),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u})|=O(\zeta+\alpha),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{u})|=O(\zeta\rho/P),

\displaystyle|\gamma_{2}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{u})|=O(\rho/P),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{u})|=\widetilde{O}\big(1/(Pn)\big).

B.3.2
Incorrect Common Feature Learning

In this part, we will study the incorrect common feature learning, i.e., quantifying the inner products
\langle\mathbf{w}_{2,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle
and
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle
.

Lemma B.5
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta=o\big(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\big)
, then recalling the update form in Proposition
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})=-\Theta(1),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})|=O(\zeta+\alpha),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})|=O(\rho/P),

\displaystyle|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})|=O(\zeta\rho/P),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v})|=\widetilde{O}\big(1/(Pn)\big).

Proof of Lemma
B.5
.

Recall the definition of
\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{2,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}.

Then comparing with the previous analysis on
\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})
, the only difference is to replace
\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}
to
\ell_{2,(i,j)}^{(t)}=-\ell_{2,(i,j)}^{(t)}
. Therefore, we can immediately get that
\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})=-\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})=-\Theta(1)
.

Regarding other terms that are bounded in terms of their absolute values, we can get the same results as in Theorem
B.3
. This completes the proof.
∎

Similarly, we can get the following results for
\mathbf{u}
.

Lemma B.6
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta=o\big(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\big)
, then recalling the update form in Proposition
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u})=-\Theta(1),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u})|=O(\zeta+\alpha),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{u})|=O(\rho/P),

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{u})|=O(\zeta\rho/P),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{u})|=\widetilde{O}\big(1/(Pn)\big).

B.3.3
Rare Feature Learning

In this part, we will study the rare feature learning, i.e., quantifying the inner products
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle
and
\langle\mathbf{w}_{2,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle
.

Lemma B.7
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta
for some
\zeta=o\big(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\big)
and
\zeta>b\alpha
, then recalling the update form in Proposition
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\Theta(\rho),\quad\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\Theta(\rho/P),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|=O(\zeta\rho/P),

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|=O(\zeta\rho^{2}/P),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|=\widetilde{O}\big(\rho/(Pn)\big).

Proof of Lemma
B.7
.

Recalling the definition of
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}.

Note that the rare feature
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
will not appear in the form of feature noise, then we will only need to focus on the mixed data
(i,j)
with either
i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{+}
or
j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{+}
, where the rare feature can only appear in the form of
\mathbf{v}
,
\lambda\mathbf{v}
, or
(1-\lambda)\mathbf{v}
. Particularly, regarding the data
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}\cup_{0,1}^{+,+}
, let
\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
be the set of patches that contain the feature
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
, we have
|\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|=\Theta(1)
and then

\displaystyle\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}=\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\cdot\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v})}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}=\Theta(1),

where we use the fact that
\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}=\Theta(1)
for any
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,+}
.

Regarding the data
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,-}
, we will consider
(i,j)
and
(j,i)
together. Particularly, we have

\displaystyle\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}\Big[[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}-[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}\Big]}_{I_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\big[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\big]\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}}_{I_{2}}.

Then using the same definition of
\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v})
, we have for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v})
, it holds that
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\lambda
and
\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=1-\lambda
, then

\displaystyle I_{1}=\Theta(1)\cdot|\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|\cdot[\lambda^{2}-(1-\lambda)^{2}]=\Theta(1).

Regarding
I_{2}
, we can use the condition that the neural network output is upper bounded by
\zeta
, then

\displaystyle|I_{2}|=\bigg|\big[\lambda-0.5+0.5-\lambda\pm O(\zeta)\big]\cdot\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{i,j}^{*}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})}[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}\bigg|=O(\zeta).

Therefore, combining these results for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we can get

\displaystyle\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}=I_{1}+I_{2}=\Theta(1).

To complete the analysis, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{+},j\in[n]||i\in[n],j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{+}}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]^{2}

\displaystyle=|{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,+}\cup_{0,1}^{+,+}|+|{\mathcal{S}}_{1,0}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,-}|

\displaystyle=\Theta(\rho).

The characterization of
\gamma_{1}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
and
\gamma_{1}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
will be exactly the same as
\gamma_{1}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})
and
\gamma_{1}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{u})
due to the fact that
\gamma_{1}(\mathbf{a},\bm{b})=\gamma_{1}(\bm{b},\mathbf{a})
. Therefore, we can apply Lemmas
B.3
and
B.6
to get the desired results.

Regarding the proof for
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
, we will follow a similar proof for
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})
in Lemma
B.3
, while two differences need to be considered: (1) the rare feature vectors
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
will not appear in the form of feature noise, thus we only need to consider the data
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{-,+}
; (2) the cardinality of the critical subset of data satisfies
|{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{-,+}|=\rho^{2}n^{2}
. Therefore, for any
(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,-}
, we have

\displaystyle\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\big[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})-\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\big]+\big[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\big]\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime}).

It is easy to see that
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=\lambda(1-\lambda)
. Besides, we have in total
\rho^{2}n^{2}/P
patches that consist of both
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
. This further implies that

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{+,-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1,1}^{-,+}}\big[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}\big]\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})\cdot\theta_{j,i}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\bigg|

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\frac{\rho^{2}}{P}\bigg)\cdot O(\zeta)

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\frac{\zeta\rho^{2}}{P}\bigg),

where we use the fact that
|\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(j,i)}^{(t)}|=O(\zeta)
.

Lastly, we will characterize
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
. First recall its definition:

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{p=q,i=s||p=q,j=s}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})}_{I_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{p\neq q||i\neq s,j\neq s}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})}_{I_{2}}.

Note that for any fixed
\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}
, it will be mixed with
n
data patches in total, while, by Lemma
B.1
, we know that there are only
\Theta(\rho/P)
fraction among them are
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
. Using the fact that
|\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}|\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{n}\cdot\Theta(\rho/P)=O\bigg(\frac{\rho}{Pn}\bigg).

Besides, note that
|\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})|=\widetilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
if
i,j\neq s
or
p\neq q
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{2}|\leq\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{\rho P}{d^{1/2}}\bigg)=O\bigg(\frac{\rho}{Pn}\bigg),

where the last equality is by the assumption that
d\geq P^{4}n^{2}
.
Combining the above results for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we can get

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|=O\bigg(\frac{\rho}{Pn}\bigg).

∎

Following the exactly same procedure, we can get the following results regarding the learning of
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
.

Lemma B.8
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta
for some
\zeta=o\big(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\big)
and
\zeta>b\alpha
, then recalling the update form in Proposition
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})=\Theta(\rho),\quad\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})=\Theta(\rho/P),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|=O(\zeta\rho/P),

\displaystyle|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|=O(\zeta\rho^{2}/P),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|=\widetilde{O}\big(\rho/(Pn)\big).

B.3.4
Incorrect Rare Feature Learning

In contrast to the previous section that studies
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle
and
\langle\mathbf{w}_{2,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle
, the incorrect rare feature learning aims to characterize the quantities
\langle\mathbf{w}_{2,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle
and
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle
. Similar to the proof of Lemmas
B.5
and
B.6
, we only need to replace
\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}
with
\ell_{2,(i,j)}^{(t)}=-\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}
or
\ell_{2,(i,j)}^{(t)}
with
\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}=-\ell_{2,(i,j)}^{(t)}
. Based on this, the update of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{2,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle
and
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle
in each iteration are characterized in the following lemmas.

Lemma B.9
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta
for some
\zeta=o\big(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\big)
and
\zeta>b\alpha
, then recalling the update form in Proposition
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=-\Theta(\rho),\quad\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})=-\Theta(\rho/P),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|=O(\zeta\rho/P),

\displaystyle|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|=O(\zeta\rho^{2}/P),\quad|\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})|=\widetilde{O}\big(\rho/(Pn)\big).

Lemma B.10
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta
for some
\zeta=o\big(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\big)
and
\zeta>b\alpha
, then recalling the update form in Proposition
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})=-\Theta(\rho),\quad\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})=-\Theta(\rho/P),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|=O(\zeta\rho/P),

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|=O(\zeta\rho^{2}/P),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}|=\widetilde{O}\big(\rho/(Pn)\big).

B.3.5
Noise Learning

Lemma B.11
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta
for some
\zeta=o\big(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\big)
and
\zeta>b\alpha
, then recalling the update form in Proposition
B.2
, for any
\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}
with
y_{s}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})=\frac{d\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot[n\lambda^{3}-(2\lambda-1)(1-\lambda)^{2}]}{2n^{2}}\pm\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})|=O\big(d\sigma_{p}^{2}/(Pn)\big),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})|=O\big(d\sigma_{p}^{2}/(Pn)\big),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})|=O\big(d\sigma_{p}^{2}\rho/(Pn)\big),

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})|=O\big(d\sigma_{p}^{2}\rho/(Pn)\big),\quad|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(q)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})|=\ind[y_{i}=y_{s}]\cdot\frac{\lambda(1-\lambda)d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}}\pm O\bigg(\frac{\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}}\bigg).

Proof of Lemma
B.11
.

Without loss of generality, we assume
y_{s}=1
. According to the definition of
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}\big(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\big)
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}\big(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})]^{2}\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\|_{2}^{2}}{n^{2}}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{i\in[n]}\ell_{1,(s,i)}^{(t)}[\theta_{s,i}^{(q)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})]^{2}+\sum_{i\neq s}\ell_{1,(i,s)}^{(t)}[\theta_{i,s}^{(q)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})]^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\|_{2}^{2}}{n^{2}}\cdot\bigg(\lambda^{2}\cdot\sum_{i\in[n]}\ell_{1,(s,i)}^{(t)}+(1-\lambda)^{2}\cdot\sum_{i\neq s}\ell_{1,(i,s)}^{(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\|_{2}^{2}}{n^{2}}\cdot\big[0.5n\lambda^{3}-(\lambda-0.5)(1-\lambda)^{2}\pm O(n\zeta)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot[n\lambda^{3}-(2\lambda-1)(1-\lambda)^{2}]}{2n^{2}}\pm O\bigg(\frac{\zeta\|\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}\bigg),

where the second equation is due to the fact that only
\mathbf{x}_{i,s}
or
\mathbf{x}_{s,i}
will contain the component of
\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}
, the fourth inequality holds since we assume there have
n/2
positive samples and
n/2
negative samples in the training data.
Moreover, note that
\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\sim N(0,\sigma_{p}^{2}\mathbf{I})
, applying union bound over all
s\in[n]
and
p\in[P]
, we can get that with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\|\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\|_{2}^{2}-d\sigma_{p}^{2}\big|\leq\mathrm{polylog}(n)\cdot d^{1/2}\sigma_{p}^{2}.

Therefore, it follows that for all
s\in[n]
and
p\in[P]
, with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}\big(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{d\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot[n\lambda^{3}-(2\lambda-1)(1-\lambda)^{2}]}{2n^{2}}\pm\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}+\frac{\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{d\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot[n\lambda^{2}-(1-\lambda)^{2}]}{2n^{2}}\pm\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg),

where we use the fact that
\zeta=\omega(d^{-1/2})
.

Regarding
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(q)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(q)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda(1-\lambda)}{n^{2}}\cdot[\ell_{1,(i,s)}^{(t)}+\ell_{1,(s,i)}^{(t)}]\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\ind[y_{i}=y_{s}]\cdot\frac{\lambda(1-\lambda)d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}}\pm O\bigg(\frac{\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}}\bigg).

Regarding the remaining quantities, we can directly apply the aforementioned lemmas on the learning of common and rare features, since the following holds

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{a},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)})=\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{a})\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)}\|_{2}^{2}=\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(q)},\mathbf{a})\cdot\widetilde{O}(d\sigma_{p}^{2}),

where
\mathbf{a}\in\{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\}
.
This completes the proof.

∎

B.4
Outcome of Phase 1 Mixup Training.

In this part, we will provide the outcome of Phase 1 mixup training.

We first recall Proposition
B.2
and Lemma
B.3
to obtain the learning dynamics of the common feature vector
\mathbf{v}
.

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle-\eta\cdot\langle\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{1,r}}L_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\big[1+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\big]\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle.

Then it can be seen that the most complicated part in the above update form is the composition of noise learning, i.e.,
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle
.The following lemma provides an upper bound on the term
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle
, which will leverage the randomness of
\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}
at the initialization.

Lemma B.12
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta
for some
\zeta\in\big[\omega\big((nP)^{-1/2}\big),o\big(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\big)\big]
. Let
z_{t}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle
, then we have with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
, for all
t=O\big(n\eta^{-1}/(d\sigma_{p}^{2})\big)
, we have

\displaystyle|z_{t}|

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{\zeta}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

Proof.

Based on the definition of
z_{t}
, we can conduct the following decomposition:

\displaystyle z_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{q\in[P]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle.

Note that during the initial training phase
\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}
is close to the constant
l_{1,(i,j)}\in\{0.5,-0.5,0.5-\lambda,\lambda-0.5\}
, which is independent of the random noise vectors
\{\bm{\xi}\}
and random initial weights
\{\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)}\}_{r\in[m]}
. Then using the fact that
|\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}-l_{1,(i,j)}|=O(\zeta)
, we can get

\displaystyle|z_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{q\in[P]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle}_{I_{1}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\bigg|\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{q\in[P]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(0)}-l_{1,(i,j)}]\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle\bigg|}_{I_{2}}.

Regarding
I_{1}
, note that
\ell_{1,(i,j)}
,
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})
, and
\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})
are independent of the random noise vectors
\{\bm{\xi}\}
and random initial weights
\{\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)}\}_{r\in[m]}
. Besides, note that the inner products
\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle\}_{i^{\prime}\in[n],q\in[P]}
are independent conditioning on
\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)}
and for all
i^{\prime}\in[n]
and
q\in[P]
, . We can apply standard concentration arguments to get the upper bound of
I_{1}
. Before approaching this, we first apply Lemma
B.1
and follow the similar proof of Lemma
B.3
, and obtain that with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\bigg|=O\bigg(\frac{1}{nP}\bigg).

(B.9)

Then performing the following decomposition on
I_{1}
according to the value of
y_{i^{\prime}}
:

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}:y_{i^{\prime}}=1}\sum_{q\in[P]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle}_{I_{1}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}:y_{i^{\prime}}=2}\sum_{q\in[P]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle}_{I_{1}^{(2)}}.

Therefore, note that conditioning on
\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)}
, the quantity
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle
is
\|\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)}\|_{2}\cdot\sigma_{p}
-subGaussian, by (
B.9
), we can immediately get that both
I_{1}^{(1)}
and
I_{1}^{(2)}
are
\|\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)}\|_{2}\cdot\sigma_{p}\cdot(nP)^{-1/2}
-subGuassian. Then using the fact that
\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)}\in N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}\mathbf{I})
, we can get that with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
,

\displaystyle|I_{1}^{(1)}|,|I_{1}^{(2)}|\leq\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{(nP)^{1/2}}\bigg).

(B.10)

Regarding
I_{2}
, we can also apply Lemma
B.1
and follow the similar proof of Lemma
B.3
, then with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(0)}-l_{1,(i,j)}]\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\bigg|=O\bigg(\frac{\max_{i,j}|\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(0)}-l_{1,(i,j)}|}{nP}\bigg)=O\bigg(\frac{\zeta}{nP}\bigg).

This further implies that

\displaystyle I_{2}\leq O\bigg(\frac{\zeta}{nP}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{q\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle|\leq\widetilde{O}\big(\zeta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\big).

(B.11)

where we use the fact that
\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)}\sim N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}\mathbf{I})
and
\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\sim N(0,\sigma_{p}^{2}\mathbf{I})
. Combining (
B.10
) and (
B.11
) leads to

\displaystyle|z_{0}|\leq\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{(nP)^{1/2}}+\zeta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\bigg)=O\big(\zeta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\big).

where we use the condition that
\zeta=\omega\big((nP)^{-1/2}\big)
.

Next we will move on to study the update of
z_{t}
using the update results of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle
in Lemma
B.11
. Particularly, we can again use the quantities
l_{1,(i,j)}
’s and get the following decomposition

\displaystyle z_{t}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{q\in[P]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle}_{I_{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{q\in[P]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\big[\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}-l_{1,(i,j)}\big]\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle}_{I_{4}}.

Recall the update results of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle
in Lemma
B.11
: for any
y_{i}=1
,

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\bigg[1+\eta\cdot\bigg(\frac{d\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot[n\lambda^{3}-(2\lambda-1)(1-\lambda)^{2}]}{2n^{2}}\pm\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}\rho}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}\rho}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\lambda(1-\lambda)d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}}\bigg)\sum_{s:y_{s}=1}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

(B.12)

For any
y_{i}=2
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\bigg[1-\eta\cdot\bigg(\frac{d\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot[n\lambda^{3}-(2\lambda-1)(1-\lambda)^{2}]}{2n^{2}}\pm\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}\rho}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}\rho}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad-O\bigg(\frac{\eta\lambda(1-\lambda)d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{s:y_{s}=1}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

(B.13)

We first prove the bound of the quantity
\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s:y_{s}=1}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle
. First, using the standard concentration result gives
|\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s:y_{s}=1}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|=\widetilde{O}\big(d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}P^{1/2}n^{1/2}\big)
. Then, by the above update rule, we can get

\displaystyle\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s:y_{s}=1}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\bigg[1+\Theta\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)\bigg]\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s:y_{s}=1}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\pm O\big(\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}\big)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\pm O\big(\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}\big)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\pm O\big(\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}\big)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\pm O\big(\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}\big)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

Then we can get that for any
t=O\big(n\eta^{-1}/(d\sigma_{p}^{2})\big)
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s:y_{s}=1}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq O\big(n^{1/2}P^{1/2}d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\big)+O(\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2})\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O(\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2})\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

Moreover, similar result can be obtained for
\sum_{s:y_{s}=2}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle
and we omit the proof here.

Now we are ready to upper bound
I_{3}
. Particularly, let
\alpha_{1}^{(t)}
and
\alpha_{2}^{(t)}
be denoted as follows:

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}:y_{i^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\sum_{q\in[P]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}:y_{i^{\prime}}=2}^{n}\sum_{q\in[P]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)})\cdot\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle.

Then it is clear that
I_{3}=\alpha_{1}^{(t)}+\alpha_{2}^{(t)}
. Then by (
B.9
) and (
B.4
),
we can get

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\bigg[1+\Theta\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)\bigg]\cdot\alpha_{1}^{(t)}\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\bigg|\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s:y_{s}=1}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

Similarly, we can also obtain

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\bigg[1-\Theta\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)\bigg]\cdot\alpha_{2}^{(t)}\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\bigg|\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s:y_{s}=2}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|\pm O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

Then using the previous results on
\big|\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s:y_{s}=1}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle\big|
and
\big|\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s:y_{s}=2}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle\big|
and (
B.10
), we can get that for any
t=O\big(n\eta^{-1}/(d\sigma_{p}^{2})\big)
,

\displaystyle|\alpha_{1}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|,

where we use the upper bound of
|\alpha_{1}^{(0)}|
provided in
Similarly, we can obtain the same results for
\alpha_{2}^{(t)}
as follows:

\displaystyle|\alpha_{2}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

Combining the above results leads to the bound of
I_{3}
.

We will finally bound
I_{4}
as follows: using the fact that
|\ell_{1,(i,j)}-l_{1,(i,j)}|=O(\zeta)
and a similar characterization of (
B.9
), we can get

\displaystyle I_{4}\leq O\bigg(\frac{\zeta}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

Combining the above bounds on
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
, we can finally get

\displaystyle|z_{t}|

\displaystyle\leq|I_{3}|+|I_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{\zeta}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

This completes the proof.

∎

Then the following lemma characterizes the growth of common feature learning.

Lemma B.13
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta
for some
\zeta=o\big(d^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}\big)
. Then for any
t=O\big(\mathrm{polylog}(n)/\eta\big)
that satisfies this condition, we have with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
, there exists at least one
r\in[m]
such that

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle=\big[1+\Theta(\eta)\big]\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle.

Proof.

First, note that
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}\rangle
follows
N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2})
, then it is easy to get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\max_{r\in[m]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\geq\sigma_{0}\bigg]=1-\big(\mathbb{P}_{\xi\sim N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2})}[|\xi|\leq\sigma_{0}]\big)^{m}\geq 1-0.7^{m}\geq 1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n),

(B.14)

where the last inequality is by our assumption that
m=\mathrm{polylog}(n)>C\log(n)
for some sufficiently large constant
C
.

Recall the update rule of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle
:

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\big[1+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\big]\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle.

Taking absolute value on both sides leads to

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|

\displaystyle\geq\big[1+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\big]\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|-\eta\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\big|-\eta\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\qquad-\eta\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle\big|-\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|.

Therefore, the next step is to show that these “negative” terms in the above inequality are dominated by
\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|
, i.e., showing that

\displaystyle\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\big|,\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\big|,\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle\big|\ll|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|;

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|\ll|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|,

where we use our result in Lemma
B.3
that
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})=\Theta(1)
. Then we are able to get that

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|

\displaystyle=\big[1+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\pm o\big(1/\mathrm{polylog}(n)\big)\big]\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\geq\big[1+\Theta(\eta)\big]\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|.

(B.15)

Regarding the first three terms, we will prove them by mathematical induction on a stronger argument (recall that
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})=\Theta(1)
,
|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})|,|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})|,|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})|=o\big(1/\mathrm{polylog}(n)\big)
, according to Lemma
B.3
): we aim to verify the hypothesis

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\big|,\quad|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\big|,\quad|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle\big|\leq c\cdot\log^{2}(n)|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|,

(B.16)

where
c
is some sufficiently small constant.

In particular, we can first consider the initialization where
t=0
, then by (
B.14
) and standard concentration bound of Gaussian random variable, we have with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
,

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|=\Omega(\sigma_{0}),\quad\big|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\big|=O\big(\log(n)\sigma_{0}\big),

\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\big|=O\big(\log(n)\sigma_{0}\big),\quad\big|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\big|=O\big(\log(n)\sigma_{0}\big).

Therefore, using the fact that
\zeta=o\big(d^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}\big)
, it is easy to verify the hypothesis. We will then assume the hypothesis holds for all
\tau\leq t
and aim to verify it for
t+1
. Particularly, recall the update rules of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\big[1-\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u})\big]\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|+\eta\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\big|+\eta\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle\big|+\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|+\eta\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big|\gamma_{1}^{(\tau)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\big|+\eta\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big|\gamma_{1}^{(\tau)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big|\gamma_{1}^{(\tau)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle\big|+\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\eta\gamma_{1}^{(\tau)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{u})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq O\big(\log(n)\sigma_{0}\big)+\widetilde{O}\big(\eta(\zeta+\alpha)\big)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho}{P}+\frac{t\rho\eta^{2}d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{t\eta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{t\eta^{2}d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta}{Pn}+\frac{t\eta^{2}\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

(B.17)

where the last inequality is by Lemma
B.12
.
Then by (
B.4
), we have the following results regarding
|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n],p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[1+O\bigg(\frac{d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n}\bigg)\bigg]\cdot\max_{i\in[n],p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle=O\big(d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\big)+O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{s=0}^{\tau}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(s)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(s)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{s=0}^{\tau}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(s)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(s)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big].

Therefore, we can accordingly get the following upper bound regarding the last term in the RHS of (
B.4
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nP}\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\max_{i\in[n],p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq O\big(td^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\big)+O\bigg(\frac{t\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{t\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big].

(B.18)

Then using the fact that
t\eta=O\big(\mathrm{polylog}(n)\big)
and
n=\omega(d\sigma_{p}^{2})
, we can further get the following on (
B.4
)

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq O\big(\log(n)\sigma_{0}\big)+\widetilde{O}\big(\eta(\zeta+\alpha)\big)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho}{P}\bigg)\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{t\eta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}+t\eta\zeta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{\eta}{P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big].

Then according to the Hypothesis
B.16
for any
\tau\leq t
, it is easy to get that

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\geq|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\geq\ldots\geq|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|.

Then we can get
|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|=\Omega(\sigma_{0})
, applying the fact that
t\eta=O(\mathrm{polylog}(n))
further gives

\displaystyle O\big(\log(n)\sigma_{0}\big)+O\bigg(\frac{t\eta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}+t\eta\zeta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]=o(\log^{2}(n)\sigma_{0})=o\big(\log^{2}(n)|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big).

(B.19)

Besides, note that the Hypothesis
B.16
holds for all
\tau\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|,|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|,|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\leq c\cdot\log^{2}(n)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\leq\log^{2}(n)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|,

(B.20)

we can immediately get that

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}\big(\eta(\zeta+\alpha)\big)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\leq\widetilde{O}\big(t\eta(\zeta+\alpha)\big)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|=o\big(\log^{2}(n)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\big)

\displaystyle O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho}{P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]\leq\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{t\eta\rho}{P}\bigg)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|=o\big(\log^{2}(n)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\big)

\displaystyle O\bigg(\frac{\eta}{P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]\leq\widetilde{O}\bigg(\frac{t\eta}{P}\bigg)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|=o\big(\log^{2}(n)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\big).

(B.21)

Putting the above results together, we can verify that

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|=o\big(\log^{2}(n)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\big).

We will then verify the Hypothesis for
\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle
. By its update rule, Lemma
B.7
, and Lemma
B.12
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\big[1+\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\big]\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+\eta\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\big|+\eta\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\big|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle\big|+\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\eta\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 2|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+\eta\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big|\gamma_{1}^{(\tau)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\big|+\eta\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big|\gamma_{1}^{(\tau)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big|\gamma_{1}^{(\tau)}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle\big|+\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\eta\gamma_{1}^{(\tau)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq O\big(\log(n)\sigma_{0}\big)+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho}{P}+\frac{t\rho\eta^{2}d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta\rho}{P}+\frac{t\eta^{2}\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta\rho^{2}}{P}+\frac{t\rho^{2}\eta^{2}d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{t\eta\rho d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho\zeta}{Pn}+\frac{t\eta^{2}\zeta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|.

Then by (
B.4
) and using the fact that
t\eta=O(\mathrm{polylog}(n))
and
n=\omega(d\sigma_{p}^{2})
, we can finally get

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq O\big(\log(n)\sigma_{0}\big)+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho}{P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho}{P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho^{2}}{P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{t\eta\rho d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}+t\eta\rho\zeta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\bigg).

Then applying (
B.19
), (
B.20
), and (
B.4
), we can also verify that

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|=o\big(\log^{2}(n)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\big).

The using exactly the same proof, we are also able to verify that

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|=o\big(\log^{2}(n)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\big).

Lastly, we will prove that

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|\leq c\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|

(B.22)

for some sufficiently small constant
c
and all
t=O\big(\mathrm{polylog}(n)/\eta\big)
. This can be proved by the combination of Lemma
B.12
, (
B.4
), and our previous characterizations (
B.19
), (
B.20
), (
B.4
). In particular, using the fact that
|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|=\Omega(\sigma_{0})
,
we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)},\mathbf{v})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{(p)}\rangle\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{\zeta}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{n}\sum_{p\in[P]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|+O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\zeta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{n^{2}P}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\sum_{p\in[P]}\sum_{s=1}^{n}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{(p)}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}+\zeta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{\eta d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\frac{\eta\rho d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\big[|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(\tau)},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\rangle|\big]

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}+\zeta d^{1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\bigg(\frac{d^{1/2}\sigma_{p}}{P^{1/2}n^{1/2}}+\zeta d^{1/2}\sigma_{p}+\frac{d\sigma_{p}^{2}}{Pn}\bigg)\cdot|\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\bigg).

(B.23)

Then using the facts that
\zeta=o(d^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1})
and
d\sigma_{p}^{2}=o(n)
, we are able to complete the proof of (
B.22
).

∎

Lemma B.14
.

Assume
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta
for some
\zeta=o\big(d^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}\big)
. Then for any
t=O\big(\mathrm{polylog}(n)/\eta\big)
that satisfies this condition, we have with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
,

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})-\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u})|\leq o\bigg(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}\bigg).

Proof.

Recall
\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}\text{ or }j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}}_{I_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\not\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}\text{ and }j\not\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}}\ell_{1,(i,j)}^{(t)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}}_{I_{2}}.

Regarding
I_{2}
, using the similar proof in Lemma
B.3
, we can obtain that
I_{2}=o\big(1/\mathrm{polylog}(n)\big)
. For
I_{1}
, using the condition that
\max_{k\in[2],(i,j)\in{\mathcal{S}}}|F_{k}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)};\mathbf{x}_{i,j})|\leq\zeta
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}\text{ or }j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}\pm O(\zeta),

where
l_{1,(i,j)}\in\{0.5,-0.5,0.5-\lambda,\lambda-0.5\}
denotes the loss derivative of data
(\mathbf{x}_{i,j},y_{i,j})
when its neural network output is forced to be zero. To this end, using the similar decomposition for
\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u})
and noting
\zeta=o(1/\mathrm{polylog}(n))
, we can obtain

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})-\gamma_{2}^{(t)}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u})|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}\text{ or }j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-}\text{ or }j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-}}l_{2,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{u})]^{2}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad+o\big(1/\mathrm{polylog}(n)\big).

(B.24)

Moreover, for any
i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}
, note that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in[P]}[\Theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}^{*}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})}[\Theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}+\sum_{j\in[n]}l_{1,(i,j)}\sum_{p\not\in\mathcal{P}^{*}_{i,j}(\mathbf{v})}[\Theta_{i,j}^{(p)}(\mathbf{v})]^{2}

\displaystyle=\ell_{1,(i,i)}+\sum_{j\neq i}l_{1,(i,j)}\cdot z_{i,j}^{2}\pm o\big(n/\mathrm{polylog}(n)\big),

where
z_{i,j}=(1-\lambda)^{2}
if
j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{-}
and

\displaystyle z_{i,j}=\begin{cases}1&\text{with probability }1/P;\\
(1-\lambda)^{2}+\lambda^{2}&\text{with probability }(P-1)/P,\end{cases}

if
j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}
.
Consequently, applying Hoeffeding’s inequality regarding the random variable
z_{i,j}
(when
j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{+}
), we have with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(n)
,
